Let's attempt to derive better local search via solving a convex QP trust region. Use active lags K = top ~n/4. We want propose δ from current a that reduces max. We can solve:
minimize  λ + (1/(2r))||δ||²
s.t. g_k^T δ ≤ τ - c_k for active k (where τ current max maybe target), 1^T δ=0, a+δ∈[0, U], δ maybe bounded.

FFT convolution for real arrays:
```
def fftconv(x,y):
    n=len(x); m=len(y); size = 1 << ((n+m-2).bit_length()) # next power of 2
    X=np.fft.rfft(x,size); Y=np.fft.rfft(y,size); return np.fft.irfft(X*Y,size)[:n+m-1]
```
Numerically small negative; clip if desired. For length 4096, size 8192. Good.

Projection onto simplex: Standard algorithm:
```
def project_simplex(v, s=1.0):
    v=np.asarray(v)
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)-1.0
    ind=np.arange(1,len(u)+1)
    cond=u-cssv/ind>0
    if not cond.any(): return zeros? # but sum can be 0? Actually for any v, at least.
    rho=ind[cond][-1]
    theta=cssv[cond][rho-1]/rho
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0)
```
Need for s=1. If v large negative, okay. For m maybe 0 handle.

Let's think performance and correctness.

Convolution function:
```
def conv_full(x,y):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); y=...
    nx,ny=len(x),len(y)
    if nx==0 or ny==0: return np.array([])
    # if small direct
    if min(nx,ny) <= 256 or (nx+ny) <= 2048? direct threshold maybe product < 1e6
        return np.convolve(x,y)
    size=1
    while size < nx+ny-1: size <<=1
    X=np.fft.rfft(x,size); Y=np.fft.rfft(y,size)
    out=np.fft.irfft(X*Y,size)[:nx+ny-1]
    # clean tiny negatives? Maybe not for grad; but c should nonnegative. Clip for score if small neg
    return out
```
For n=4096, product 16M direct maybe okay once but many not. Use FFT if nx*ny > 200000 maybe.

For response h = ∑ w_k a_{k-i}: We can use same conv_full(a, w[::-1]) and slice.
```
def response_from_weights(w,a):
    # w length 2n-1, a length n
    if len(w)==0: return zeros
    full = conv_full(a, w[::-1])
    return full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Check length: full length n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2. Indices n-1 to 2n-2 inclusive length n. Good. For n=1, full length 1? 3n-2=1, n-1:2n-1 =0:1, ok.

Gradient of smooth objective:
```
def smooth_objgrad(x): # x log var
    a=np.exp(x - logsumexp(x)); # but x may large; use softmax stable
    c=conv_full(a,a)
    # lse
    m=c.max()
    if beta<=0: ...
    z=beta*(c-m)
    ez=np.exp(z)
    s=ez.sum()
    val = m + np.log(s)/beta
    w=ez/s
    h=response_from_weights(w,a)
    grad_a=2*h
    grad_x=a*(grad_a - np.dot(a,grad_a))
    return val, grad_x
```

mx=x.max(); ex=np.exp(x-mx); a=ex/ex.sum()
```

Flat dual optimizer details.

Function `flatten_dual_optimize(a, time_budget, seed, max_K=...)`:
- Input a normalized.
- best = a, bestsc=score.
- rng.
- K schedule: start min(128,max(32,n//8)), increase if stuck.
- loop until time:
   - active top K.
   - solve flat λ (qp_iters=30)
   - dir = response - mean; std = norm; if std tiny break/stall.
   - line search eta: normalize d=dir/std. Actually if std small, flat, try perturb.
   - candidates: for eta in geometric 0.05..10: cand = normalize(a * exp(-eta*d)); score; best if improve. Also maybe +eta.
   - if improve: update a,best, stuck=0; maybe increase K? If stuck: noise or new active K.
- Return best.

Need line search efficient: 20 evals * conv. For n=512, okay. We can reduce to 15. Use beta smooth? exact score conv. For each eta, computing conv O(n log n). 15* maybe 50 iterations =750 conv; fine.

Choice of eta: d has std 1. Multiplicative step size in log. For a near uniform, log changes η; if η=1, a varies factor e. Could be too aggressive. We can line search log η from -4 to 3. Use if score finite. If no improvement, try smaller. Since current a may be local, small steps improve.

The flat dual QP solve:
```
def solve_flat_lambda(a, active, qp_iters=40):
    m=len(active); n=len(a)
    if m==0: return None,None
    lam = np.ones(m)/m or c based
    # maybe initialize with c[active]/sum
    step = 1.0
    last_obj = inf
    for it in range(qp_iters):
       w = np.zeros(2*n-1); w[active]=lam
       h = response(w,a)
       hcenter = h - h.mean()
       obj = float(np.dot(hcenter,hcenter))
       if obj < 1e-20: break
       # gradient under f=||hcenter||^2? Centering makes gradient projection: ∇ = 2 G^T hcenter (since mean term zero derivative due 1^T Gλ? But 1^T Gλ = ∑_k∑_i λ_k a_{k-i}=∑_k λ_k ∑ a = ∑λ=1, constant, so derivative of mean =0. Good.)
       t = conv_full(hcenter, a) # length 2n-1
       grad = 2*t[active]
       # step by projection with backtracking simple
       new_lam = None
       for _ in range(8):
           cand = project_simplex(lam - step*grad)
           # compute obj_cand maybe approximate
           w2 = zeros; w2[active]=cand
           h2 = response(w2,a); hc2=h2-h2.mean(); obj2=dot
           if obj2 <= obj + 1e-12*np.max((1,obj)):
                new_lam=cand; new_obj=obj2; break
           step *=0.5
       if new_lam is None:
           step=1.0
           cand=project_simplex(lam - 1e-6*np.linalg.norm(grad)*grad/ (norm?**))
           new_lam=cand; new_obj=obj
       lam=new_lam
       if abs(new_obj-obj) < 1e-14: break
       # adapt step up if converged? if new_obj < obj: step=min(step*1.1, upper)
    return lam, hcenter
```
But computing objective for backtrack calls response each time; 8*40 heavy. We can instead use fixed step and no backtrack, or accept if new_obj <= obj + small else keep. Simpler and robust:
- Estimate Lipschitz bound L = 2 * m * S2^2? Let S2=∑a². G columns norm² ≤ S2. Then ||G||₂² ≤ m*S2. L=2||G||⁴? For f=||Gλ||², gradient=2G^TGλ, Lipschitz =2||G^TG||² =2||G||⁴ ≤2(m*S2)². Step=1/L. If S2 small, step huge; cap step ≤ 1.0/m? Actually λ changes; scale. Projected gradient stable with step=1/L. If S2=0.01,m=128,L=2*(1.28)^2? Wait m*S2=1.28; L≈3.3, step0.3. OK. If S2=1 (point mass), L=32768, step small. But point mass score huge not our case. Step okay. We can use `step = min(1.0, 1.0 / max(1e-12, 2.0 * (m * s2) ** 2))`? If S2 ~0.003? FFT:
```
def _fft_size(m):
    if HAVE_SCIPY_FT:
        return int(next_fast_len(m))
    size=1
    while size<m: size<<=1
    return size
```
But next_fast_len imported? use try.

`fft_convolve(x,y)`:
```

out=irfft(X*Y, size)
if out.size > len(x)+len(y)-1: out=out[:len]
return out.real
```

# objective function objective(h): r = np.zeros(n); r[support]=h[support]-h[support].mean(); return dot(r,r), r

    w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[active]=lam
    h=response_weights(w,a)
    if n_s < n:

        mean=float(np.mean(hsup)) if hsup.size else 0
        r=np.zeros(n); r[support]=hsup-mean
    else:
        mean=float(h.mean()); r=h-mean
    obj=float(np.dot(r,r))
    if obj < 1e-18: break
    if obj < best_obj: best_obj=obj; best_lam=lam.copy(); best_r=r.copy()
    t=convolve_full(r,a)
    grad=2.0*t[active]
    gnorm=float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
    if gnorm <= 1e-16: break
    # adaptive step: start step_estimate = 1.0 / (2.0 * m * s2?) but use 1.0
    # Use projected gradient with backtracking maybe 4 tries.
    accepted=False
    for s in [step, 0.5*step, 0.25*step, 0.1*step, 0.01*step]:
         cand=project_simplex(lam - s*grad)
         # quick objective
         w2=zeros; w2[active]=cand; h2=response...; r2... obj2
         if obj2 <= obj + 1e-12*max(1,obj):
             lam=cand; step=min(s*1.25, 2.0); accepted=True; break
    if not accepted:
         step*=0.25
         cand=project_simplex(lam - step*grad)
         lam=cand # even if slight increase? maybe keep if no better? Better keep if not increase? Use if obj2 < obj? If no, skip.
    if it > 10 and abs(obj-best_obj)<1e-16: break
return best_lam, best_r
```
This may call many response. QP dimension small. We can reduce backtracking: use fixed step based on s2:
```

```
Let's derive safe L upper: f(λ)=||P G λ||². G columns are B_k a. Norm² col =∑_{support?} (a_{k-i})² ≤ S2_all =∑a². P reduces norm. ||PG||₂² ≤ m*S2. L(f)=2||PG||⁴ ≤2(m*S2)². Step=1/L. Use `step = min(2.0, 1.0 / max(1e-12, 2.0*(m*s2)**2))`. If step too small, many iterations no move. Could use backtracking with initial step=1.0 and reduce; since m scale, step=1 often okay? Maybe solve λ with fewer iterations and no backtracking. Use fixed step=1.0/(1+2*m*s2^2) but no objective eval for accept. We just iterate lam=project(lam - step*grad). Implement `solve_flat_lambda` with fixed step and 30 iterations:
```
def solve_flat_lambda(a, active, support, c_active, qp_iters=30):
   n=len(a); m=len(active)
   lam = c_active / c_active.sum() if sum>0 else ones/m
   s2 = float(np.dot(a,a))
   step = 1.0 / max(1e-15, 2.0 * (m * max(s2,1e-12)) ** 2)
   step = min(2.0, max(step, 1e-8))
   best_lam=lam.copy(); best_r=None; best_obj=inf
   for it in range(qp_iters):
      w=zeros; w[active]=lam
      h=response_weights(w,a)
      if support_count < n:
         hsup=h[support]; mean=mean; r=zeros; r[support]=hsup-mean
      else:
         r=h-h.mean()
      obj=dot(r,r)
      if obj < best_obj: best_obj=obj; best_lam=lam.copy(); best_r=r.copy()
      if obj < 1e-20 or it == qp_iters-1: break
      t=convolve_full(r,a)
      grad=2.0*t[active]
      # remove mean? No λ simplex projection.
      lam = project_simplex(lam - step*grad)
   return best_lam, best_r
```
If r best None (should not), return current.

If using support only, r zeros outside support, H =? G columns but P_support zeros outside; ||col||² over support ≤ S2_support ≤ S2_total. Thus ||G||² ≤ m*S2_total. def flat_dual_optimize(a0, budget, seed):
   a=normalize(a0); best=a; bestsc=score(a)
   t0=time; deadline=t0+budget
   rng=default_rng(seed)
   K0 = int(min(max(64, n//4), 256)) maybe for 512=128,1024=256,2048=256? Use cap 512.
   K=K0; qp_iters=30
   stuck=0
   it=0
   while time<deadline-5:
       c=conv_same(a); M=max
       active=active_indices(c,K,32)
       support=a>max(1e-14,1e-10*max(a)); if support count<max(16,n//50)? use top? maybe all? Use all if support_count<n//2? To avoid zeros. `if support.sum() < max(10, n//10): support = np.ones(n,bool)`
       lam, r = solve_flat_lambda(a, active, support, c[active], qp_iters)
       if r is None or norm(r) tiny:
           # add noise/restart
       d = r/ (norm over support)
       line search...
       if improved:
          a=best_candidate; best...
          stuck=0
          # increase K every 50 improvements?
       else:
          stuck+=1
          if stuck>20:
             # perturb a by noise with sigma decaying; or new active more
             noise=exp(rng.normal(0, 0.1, n)); a=normalize(best*noise); sc=score; if not improve? Use best as base but current explore
             stuck=0
          # adjust K
       it+=1
       print maybe every 50
   return best,bestsc
```

def lp_m_step(a, seed, max_rows=None, use_active=True):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
   a=normalize(a); n=len(a); c=conv_same(a); M=max
   if max_rows is None: max_rows = 2*n-1 if n<=1024 else 1024
   if use_active and max_rows < 2*n-1:
       active = np.argpartition(c, -max_rows)[-max_rows:]
   else active=np.arange(2*n-1)
   K=len(active); A=np.zeros((K,n))
   for idx,k in enumerate(active):
       s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(k,n-1)
       if s<=e:
          j=np.arange(s,e+1)
          A[idx,j]=a[k-j]
   cvec=-np.ones(n); cvec += tiny noise
   bvec=np.full(K, M*(1+1e-8))
   bounds=[(0,1)]*n
   res=linprog(cvec,A_ub=A,b_ub=bvec,bounds=bounds,method='highs',options={'presolve':True})
   if res success: g=normalize(res.x); line search with t 0.1..1:
   return best candidate
```

Line search function:
```
def line_search_direction(a, r, seed, max_evals=18, support=None):
    # r centered direction (to subtract). scale
    rs = r.copy()
    mask = support if support is not None else rs!=0
    if mask.sum()==0: return a,score(a),0
    vals = r[mask]; std=np.sqrt(np.mean(vals*vals))+1e-300
    d = r/std
    best=a; bestsc=local_score(a); best_eta=0
    etas = np.geomspace(0.02, 20.0, 16) maybe include 0.0, 0.01
    for eta in etas:
       b=normalize(a*np.exp(-np.clip(eta*d,-50,50)))
       if b is None: continue
       sc=local_score(b)
       if sc<bestsc: update
       # additive maybe if eta small:
       b2=normalize(np.clip(a - (eta/10)*d,0,None))
       sc2=...
    return best,bestsc,besteta,d
```

Let's explore: At local optimum, active A(λ) a ≈ c 1. We can choose a to make response as flat as possible (minimize variance of A a) while sum1, a≥0. This is a convex quadratic? Objective ||A a - mean(Aa)||² = a^T A^T P A a. A = ∑ λ_k B_k is a banded symmetric? B_k symmetric; A symmetric. But P A may not be PSD? Objective variance of linear function; A a linear (since A matrix times a). Wait A a = response h with weights λ. h = A a is linear in a! Indeed for fixed λ, h_i =∑_k λ_k a_{k-i} = (A a)_i. A is a banded symmetric matrix with nonnegative entries. Objective minimize variance of A a over simplex. This is convex quadratic (A^T P A PSD). Constraints linear. We can solve! This is much better than multiplicative with h depending on a nonlinearly. We can alternate:
1. Given a, compute active and λ (flat or weights).
2. Solve convex QP: minimize variance h(a) = A a - mean(Aa) subject a≥0, sum a=1 (maybe bounds). This yields a_new that makes dual response flat given current λ.
3. Update a, repeat.
This is a majorization/minimization; could converge to stationary KKT. QP with n variables and banded A can be solved efficiently via scipy minimize L-BFGS/SLSQP or specialized projected gradient. Objective smooth convex, can use projected gradient on simplex. This may outperform multiplicative.

Let's detail. For λ over active set (nonnegative sum1), define matrix A (n x n) with A[i,k?] =? h_i =∑_k λ_k a_{k-i}. This is convolution of a with λ? It is linear. The matrix B(λ) is banded with at most n nonzero per row. We can apply B(λ) to a via `response_weights(λ_extended, a)` (FFT). Objective F(a)=||P B a||². We want minimize over simplex. Gradient wrt a: ∇F = 2 B^T P B a. But B^T =? Since B is symmetric? B_i,j = λ_{i+j}? Valid if i+j = k. This is symmetric matrix (B[i,j]=B[j,i]=λ_{i+j} if valid). P sym. So B^T P = B P (if B symmetric). Gradient =2 B (P h)? Wait derivative of ||B a - mean||²: B a - mean linear; derivative =2 B^T P (B a) =2 B P h. We can compute P h = r; then B r = response_weights(λ, r). So gradient via one response. Great! We can run projected gradient or L-BFGS on simplex for convex QP. This is attractive.

We can solve QP approximately with projected gradient/FISTA since n up to 4096, objective smooth. Hessian PSD, Lipschitz can be bounded. We can use projected gradient with adaptive steps, or use `scipy.optimize.minimize` L-BFGS-B on softmax parameter for this convex QP. But we can formulate directly with simplex projection; PG cheap.

This "flat response QP" can be the core algorithm. It is different from previous and likely more effective.

Algorithm alternating:
- Current a.
- Compute c, active top K.
- Choose λ. Options: uniform over active, proportional c, or solve flat λ from current a as before. But for QP, λ can be any nonnegative over active; we can choose based on c (e.g. top K with weights c/M or uniform). The true KKT λ is unknown; active lags with c near max. We can use weights proportional to c or solve flat λ on current a (iteratively). But QP changes a; λ should adapt. We can alternate:
   a) Given a, choose active top K and λ = solve_flat_lambda(a, active) (min variance h in λ-space). This λ aims flat for current a.
   b) Given λ, solve QP for a to minimize variance B a. This may make even flatter.
Repeat. This is two-level convex subproblem. Should work.

We can also simply choose λ proportional to active c and solve QP. Simpler: At each iteration, active top K; λ = c[active]/sum; solve QP for a. No need flat λ QP? But KKT λ can be any in normal cone; proportional c maybe okay. The QP for a with fixed λ is convex and can significantly improve. After QP, recompute c and active.

Let's test logic: Starting from parent, active near max. λ weights high lags. QP minimizes variance of h =B a over simplex with λ weights. It effectively moves a to reduce weighted active convolution variations, not necessarily true max. Then active changes. This is like bundle method. Should be robust.

Can we solve QP more directly? Minimize variance h over simplex. Since h linear, variance = ||B a||² - (1^T B a)²/n. 1^T B a =∑_i h_i =∑_k λ_k ∑_i a_{k-i} =∑_k λ_k S_k, where S_k=sum valid a (≤1). This is linear in a (because S_k =∑_i a_{k-i} = sum valid a; for each k, a dot vector of ones valid). We can maybe add constraint to enforce S_k? Not needed.

Projected gradient on simplex for F(a). Need Lipschitz bound. B banded with weights sum1. Operator norm ||B||₂ ≤? B symmetric nonnegative with row sums ≤1? Row i sum_j B[i,j]=∑_{k valid} λ_k = total λ over k=i+{0..n-1} ≤1. So row sums ≤1, but operator norm ≤ max row sum ≤1 (for nonnegative symmetric? spectral norm ≤ max row sum). Actually B entries nonnegative, symmetric, row sum ≤1. Then ||B||₂ ≤1. P ≤1. Lipschitz for variance objective: H=2B^T P B, L=2||H||/2? For F=||P Ba||² = a^T B^T P B a. Gradient=2B^T P B a. Lipschitz =2||B^T P B|| ≤2||B||² ≤2. Nice! Since row sums ≤1. If λ sum1 and weights nonnegative, B is a doubly? Row sums ≤1, col sums ≤1. So L≤2. Great! Step size 1/L =0.5 safe. Very good. We can run projected gradient with step 0.5 or use FISTA step 1 (L=2? for gradient step step=1/L=1/2). FISTA with L=2 step=1/2. This is efficient!

Let's verify row sum ≤1: B[i,j]=λ_{i+j} if 0≤i+j≤2n-2. For fixed i, j=0..n-1, k=i+j ranges i..i+n-1. Sum λ over active subset in that range ≤ total λ=1. Yes. Symmetric. Operator norm of symmetric nonnegative with maximum row sum ≤1: ||B||∞≤1, and for symmetric ||B||₂≤sqrt(||B||₁||B||∞)=√(≤1*≤1)=1. Good. P idempotent norm1. Thus L=2||B^T P B|| ≤2. Excellent.

Thus QP subproblem can be solved with projected gradient/FISTA easily, each iteration: h = B a (response), r=h-mean, grad=2 B r (response of r with λ), step=0.5 (or FISTA momentum). Project onto simplex. This is O(FFT) per QP iter. 50 QP iters per outer -> cheap.

- a0 current projected simplex (sum1).
- y=a
- t=1
- for iter:

   grad = 2*response(λ, ry)  # B^T P B y

Implement outer loop `bundle_qp_optimize`:
```
def bundle_qp_optimize(a0, budget, seed):
   a=normalize(a0); best=a; bestsc=score
   n=len(a); K=...
   while time<deadline:
       c=conv_same(a); M=max
       active=active_indices(c,K,32)
       cact=c[active]; sumcact
       lambdas=[]
       if sumcact>0: lambdas.append(cact/sumcact)
       lambdas.append(np.ones(m)/m)
       # optional flat lambda if time: solve_flat_lambda(a, active, support, cact, iters=20)
       for lam in lambdas (maybe with noise):
           w=zeros; w[active]=lam
           a_qp=qp_variance_simplex(a, w, iters=30)
           # line search t=1,0.5,...
           for t in [1,0.7,0.4,0.2]:
               b=normalize((1-t)*a + t*a_qp)
               sc=score
               if sc<best: update
       if improved: stuck=0 else stuck++
       if stuck: noise/new active
   return best
```

Let's implement alternating KKT solver:
```
def kkt_optimize(a0, budget, seed):
   a=normalize(a0); best...
   K=...
   while budget:
      c=conv_same(a); M=max
      active=topK
      # 1. solve for lambda that makes current gradients flat
      lam = solve_flat_lambda(a, active, c[active], iters=20)
      if lam is None: lam = c[active]/sum
      # 2. solve a-QP for fixed lam
      lam_q = lam
      # maybe mix with uniform/c weights
      a_qp = qp_variance_simplex(a, w_from(lam_q), iters=40)
      # line search blend
      accept best
      if no improve: try lam = c active, uniform, random; a_qp for each.
```
This is more systematic.

Now detailed functions.

`convolve_full` with direct threshold:
```
def convolve_full(x,y):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); y=np.asarray(y,float)
    nx=len(x); ny=len(y)
    if nx==0 or ny==0: return np.zeros(0)
    if nx*ny <= 400000:
        out=np.convolve(x,y)
        return out
    size=_fft_len(nx+ny-1)
    X=rfft(x,size); Y=rfft(y,size)
    out=irfft(X*Y,size)
    return out[:nx+ny-1]
```
If using scipy.fft, set workers? no.

`solve_lambda_flat(a, active, cact, iters=20)`:
- n=len(a); m=len(active)
- If m==0: None
- Initialize lam = cact if sum>0 else ones/m. But cact may be huge scale; normalize. If sum very small, ones.
- s2 = dot(a,a); step = 1/(2*(m*max(s2,1e-12))**2); step=min(2.0,max(step,1e-10))
- best_lam=lam; best_obj=inf; best_hc=None
- for it in range(iters):
   w=zeros(2n-1); w[active]=lam
   h=response_weights(w,a)
   hc=h-h.mean()
   obj=dot(hc,hc)
   if obj < best_obj: best...
   if obj < 1e-24: break
   t=convolve_full(hc,a)  # length 2n-1
   grad=2.0*t[active]
   lam_new=project_simplex(lam - step*grad)
   if np.array_equal? if allclose(lam_new, lam): break
   lam=lam_new
return best_lam, best_hc

    hc=hy-hy.mean()

    # If grad norm tiny break
    gn=float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
    if gn < 1e-12: break

    # optional: if x sum off, normalize
    tn=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t))
    y=x + ((t-1)/tn)*(x-a)
    y=y/y.sum() if sum>0 else x
    a=x
    t=tn
- return normalize(a)

Line search blend:
```
def blend_best(a,b, ts=(1.0,0.75,0.5,0.25,0.1)):
   best=a; bestsc=local_score(a)
   if b is None: return...
   for t in ts:
       c=(1-t)*a + t*b; c=normalize(c)
       if c is None: continue
       sc=local_score(c)
       if sc<bestsc: best=c; bestsc=sc
   return best,bestsc
```

def kkt_optimize(a0, budget, seed, K=None):
   t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget
   a=normalize(a0); if a is None: return None,inf
   # floor
   a = np.maximum(a,1e-15); a=normalize(a)
   best=a.copy(); bestsc=local_score(best)
   n=len(a)
   if K is None: K=int(min(max(80, n//4), 512))
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   lam_iters = 20 if n<=1024 else 10
   qp_iters = 40 if n<=2048 else 60
   stuck=0; it=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
       c=conv_same(a); M=float(np.max(c))
       if not finite or M<=0: break
       active=active_top(c, K, 32)
       cact=c[active]
       candidates=[]
       # lambda types
       # 1 flat lambda best
       lam, _ = solve_lambda_flat(a, active, cact, lam_iters)
       if lam is not None: candidates.append(lam)
       # 2 c weights
       s=cact.sum()
       if s>0: candidates.append(cact/s)
       # 3 uniform
       candidates.append(np.ones(len(active))/len(active))
       # maybe random dirichlet every 10
       if it % 10 == 7:
           candidates.append(np.exp(rng.normal(size=len(active))))
       # To limit, take first 3 or if time.
       rem=deadline-time; if rem<2: break
       per_mode_gallery? For each candidate (max 3):
           w=zeros; w[active]=lam
           # adjust qp_iters based on rem: iters = int(np.clip(rem/ (n?)))? Use fixed.
           a_qp=qp_fix_lambda(a, w, qp_iters)
           b2,b2sc=blend_best(a,a_qp,(1.0,0.6,0.3,0.1))
           if b2sc<bestsc: best=b2; bestsc=b2sc; a=best.copy(); stuck=0; print; break? If one improves, use it and continue.
           # If no improve, try next lambda
       if stuck increment if no improve this outer
       if stuck > 15:
           # noise
           sigma = 0.1 + 0.2*rng.random()
           noise=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,n))
           a=normalize(best * noise)
           sc=local_score(a)
           if sc < bestsc: bestsc=sc; best=a.copy()
           # Also add uniform floor
           a=np.maximum(a,1e-15); normalize
           stuck=0
       # Optionally change K every 20 stuck
       it+=1
   return best,bestsc
```

Implement smooth function robustly:
```
def smooth_value_grad(x, beta):
    a=softmax(x)
    c=conv_same(a)
    mfloat=float(np.max(c))
    z=beta*(c-mfloat)
    ez=np.exp(z)
    s=ez.sum()
    if s<=0: ...
    val=mfloat + np.log(s)/beta
    w=ez/s
    h=response_weights(w,a)
    grad_a=2.0*h
    gdot=float(np.dot(a,grad_a))
    grad_x=a*(grad_a - gdot)
    return val, grad_x

def smooth_minimize(a0, budget, seed):
   if not HAVE_MIN: return
   t0; deadline
   best=a0; bestsc=score
   x=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300))
   betas=(4,10,25,60,150)
   for beta in betas:
      rem=deadline-time; if rem<20: break
      # estimate per fun: call once
      st=time; v,g=smooth_value_grad(x,beta); per=max(1e-5,(time-st)*3)
      alloc=min(rem-10, 60) maybe increasing with beta? per beta allocate 30s.
      maxfun=int(min(2000, max(50, alloc/per)))
      maxiter=maxfun
      try:
         res=minimize(smooth_value_grad, x, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'gtol':1e-8, 'ftol':1e-12})
      except: break
      a_new=softmax(res.x)
      sc=local_score(a_new)
      if sc<bestsc: best=a_new; bestsc=sc; update x=res.x
      else:
         # maybe still use x? Keep x=res.x if smooth val lower? Could help. Use res.x clipped.
      x=np.clip(res.x if hasattr(res,'x') else x, -80, 80)
   return best,bestsc
```

Implement `lp_step(a, seed, max_rows=None, max_iters_t?)` single LP and line search:
```
def lp_step(a, seed):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return a, local_score(a)
   n=len(a); if n>1500: return a, score
   c=conv_same(a); M=max
   if M<=0: return...
   max_rows = 2*n-1 if n<=512 else 1024
   if max_rows >=2*n-1: active=np.arange(2*n-1)
   else: active=np.argpartition(c, -max_rows)[-max_rows:]
   K=len(active)
   A=np.zeros((K,n))
   for idx,k in enumerate(active):
       s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(k,n-1)
       if s<=e:
          j=np.arange(s,e+1,dtype=int)
          A[idx,j]=a[k-j]
   rng=...; cvec=-np.ones(n); cvec+=1e-8*rng.normal(size=n)
   bvec=np.full(K, M*(1+1e-8))
   res=linprog(cvec,A_ub=A,b_ub=bvec,bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
   if res success:
      g=normalize(res.x)
      return blend_best(a,g)
```
Bounds `(0,None)` because f*g constraints with sum? Previous used (0,1), but if a values sum1 ~1/n, g maybe >1? Since f normalized, if g up to∞, LP maybe unbounded? Constraints f*g≤M finite and g nonnegative; sum g bounded? For f positive, yes maybe bounded. But if some f zeros? Could be unbounded. Use bound [0,1] safe. Since a after normalize max maybe <1; g bound1. Use [0,1]. Good.

Candidate generation:
```
def make_candidate(n, seed, parent=None):
   rng=default_rng(seed)
   kind=int(rng.integers(0,5))
   if parent is not None and kind<3:
      p=parent if parent.size>1 else None
      if p is not None:
         # interpolation
         old=np.linspace(0,1,p.size); new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
         val=np.interp(new,old,p)
         # maybe block resample? 
         sigma=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.6))
         val=val*np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,n))
         val=np.maximum(val,1e-12); return normalize(val)
   # random
   if kind==0:
      val=np.ones(n)*np.exp(rng.normal(0,float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.8)),n))
   elif kind==1:
      z=np.random.choice([-1,1],n)
      # enforce anti-sym
      if n%2==0:
         for i in range(n//2): z[n-1-i] = -z[i]
      else:
         z[n//2]=0
         for i in range(n//2): z[n-1-i] = -z[i]
      alpha=float(rng.uniform(0.3,0.8))
      val=(1+alpha*z)/n
   elif kind==2:
      nb=int(rng.integers(max(4,n//64), n//16+1)); nb=max(1, min(nb,n))
      heights=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,nb)); heights=heights/heights.mean()
      val=np.repeat(heights, n//nb+1)[:n]
      val=val*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2,n))
   elif kind==3:
      val=rng.beta(2,2,n)
   else:
      val=np.ones(n)
   val=np.maximum(val,1e-12); return normalize(val)
```
Loops for anti-sym in Python for n up to 4096 okay. Can vectorize:

if n%2==0:

  idx1=np.arange(n//2); idx2=n-1-idx1; z[idx2] = -z[idx1]
else:
  z[n//2]=0; idx1=np.arange(n//2); idx2=n-1-idx1; z[idx2] = -z[idx1]
```

Now, let's think about correctness of KKT QP in more detail with nonnegative and support. If a has zeros, KKT condition constant only on support. Our QP uses variance over all n, forcing B a flat at zeros too. This may be too restrictive and prevent zero support. If optimal has zeros, using all n could converge to a positive sequence with higher score. We floor to positive small, so all positive; if optimal zeros, we approximate with small values. Flat over all n may be okay if floor tiny? But zeros contribute to mean, perhaps shifting q. Could be suboptimal. We can modify QP objective to variance over support where a > floor, not all. But then B matrix with P_support; operator norm still ≤1, gradient response(w, r) with r zero outside support works. FISTA step bound same (P_support norm≤1). We can implement QP with support mask dynamic each outer? If a changes, support changes. In `qp_fix_lambda`, we can pass `mask`. Objective F(a)=||P_S B a||². Gradient =2 B^T P_S B a = 2 B (P_S B a) (B symmetric). P_S zero outside and subtract mean over S: r_i = (B a)_i - mean_S for i∈S, else 0. This can be computed: h=response(w,y); if mask: hsup=h[mask]; mean=hsup.mean(); r=zeros; r[mask]=hsup-mean. Then grad=2*response(w,r). Bound L: P_S norm ≤1, B norm≤1 still. Good.

Using support mask may allow zeros. But if mask size small, objective can become zero with B a constant on support; score maybe okay. However if mask excludes many indices, QP won't move mass to outside zero support; floor may allow. We can set support = a > floor where floor = max(1e-10, 1e-12*max(a)). This may be all if floor tiny. If parent has true zeros, floor 1e-10 * max ~1e-12, support maybe indices > that. Such zeros remain zeros in QP, preventing new support. But random noise/noise perturb can add. Alternatively use all n for QP to allow mass everywhere. For near-optimal, perhaps all positive. Which is better? Previous parent likely positive? Not sure. To escape, use all n in QP by default; but can support-mask as variant. Simpler: use all n in QP to let mass flow to any index. LD: `r=h-mean all`. This enforces flat including zeros; if starting with zeros, QP may increase mass there to flatten, good. If optimal has zeros, all-n flat may slightly raise them, small cost. Use all-n primary. Maybe optional support variant if stuck? Keep code simpler all-n.

`project_simplex(v)` algorithm:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).ravel()
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return v.copy()
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)-1.0
    ind=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
    cond = (u - cssv/ind) > 0
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.zeros(n)
    rho = int(ind[cond][-1])
    theta = cssv[cond][rho-1]/rho
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
This projects onto sum=1. If v has nan? avoid. Good.

`smooth_value_grad` softmax:
```
def softmax(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    m=np.max(x)
    ex=np.exp(x-m)
    s=ex.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return np.ones(x.size)/x.size
    return ex/s
```
If x has huge negative, exp underflow to 0 for all? If x all -1e300, subtract max 0? max=-1e300, x-m=0 for max, okay. If multiple -inf? not. Good.

`convolve_full` if using scipy.next_fast_len: Need import. Code:
```
try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft as _rfft, irfft as _irfft, next_fast_len as _next_len
    _HAVE_SCIPY_FT=True
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft as _rfft, irfft as _irfft
    _HAVE_SCIPY_FT=False
```
Then `_fft_len`:
```
def _fft_len(m):
    if m <=0: return 1
    if _HAVE_SCIPY_FT:
       return int(_next_len(m))
    size=1
    while size < m: size <<=1
    return size
```
For numpy, power of two.

`convolve_full`: If direct threshold, `np.convolve` handles. If FFT, scipy irfft returns size maybe size; if size not power of two okay. Clip tiny negative? For score, we can `out = np.maximum(out,0.0)`? But for gradients, negative tiny could matter? It's okay to clip? If true convolution nonnegative, clipping improves. But response h with weights nonnegative and a nonnegative should nonnegative; FFT roundoff negative tiny. Clipping can slightly bias but small. We'll clip in local_score maybe not in response. For response, `hy = np.maximum(hy,0.0)` maybe if tiny negative. But gradients need real. Use no clip except local_score. In QP, tiny negative could make objective slightly; not issue.

`local_score`: Use `c=conv_same(a); c=np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0); m=float(np.max(c)); if m<0: m=0`. Scale: `s=float(np.sum(a)); if s <= 1e-300 return inf; return 2*n*m/(s*s)`. Good.

`normalize_sequence`:
```
def normalize_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return None
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=1e-300: return None
    a=a/s
    a=np.minimum(a,1000.0) # not needed
    return a
```
Clip to [0,None]. If we cap 1000 after normalize sum may change? Don't.

`response_weights`:
```
def response_weights(w,a):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    w=np.asarray(w,float)
    if w.size==0 or np.all(w==0): return np.zeros(n)
    full=convolve_full(a, w[::-1])
    # full length 3n-2
    start=n-1; stop=2*n-1
    if full.size < stop: # fallback pad?
       full=np.pad(full,(0,stop-full.size))
    return full[start:stop][::-1]
```
Check slice when n=1: start=0, stop=1, full size? a len1, w len1, conv full len1, full[0:1][::-1] len1. Good.

`active_top`:
```
def active_top(c,K,min_count=16):
    L=len(c)
    if L==0: return np.array([],int)
    K=int(min(max(1,K),L))
    idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    if len(idx) < min_count:
        min_count=int(min(min_count,L))
        idx=np.argpartition(c, -min_count)[-min_count:]
    return np.sort(idx.astype(int))
```
If K < min_count? We set K max. Good.


`solve_lambda_flat`: Need ensure `active` int array. `cact` align. `lam` normalized. If cact sum not finite. Use `np.clip` and normalize.
```
def solve_lambda_flat(a, active, cact, iters=20):
    n=len(a); m=active.size
    if m==0: return None,None
    cact=np.asarray(cact,float)
    if not finite: cact=ones
    s=sum
    if s>0: lam=cact/s else ones
    lam=project_simplex(lam)
    s2=float(np.dot(a,a))
    step=1.0 / max(1e-15, 2.0*(m*max(s2,1e-12))**2)
    step=min(2.0, max(1e-8, step))
    best_lam=lam; best_obj=inf; best_hc=None
    w=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    for it in range(iters):
       w[active]=lam
       h=response_weights(w,a)
       hc=h-h.mean()
       obj=float(np.dot(hc,hc))
       if obj<best_obj: best...
       if obj<1e-24: break
       t=convolve_full(hc,a)
       grad=2.0*t[active]
       lam_new=project_simplex(lam - step*grad)
       if np.allclose(lam_new,lam,rtol=1e-14,atol=1e-14): break
       lam=lam_new
    return best_lam,best_hc
```
Potential: w should be reset zeros each iteration? We assign lam to active, but if active changes? within function active fixed. But w from previous iteration retains lam values at active; okay. If active size m, all active assigned. No need zero. However if we early break, best_hc corresponds. Good.

But if active has repeated? no.

`qp_fix_lambda`:
```
def qp_fix_lambda(a0, w, iters=40, step=0.5):
    a=normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a is None: return np.zeros(len(a0))
    n=a.size
    if w.size != 2*n-1: 
       # if mismatch, try?
    y=a.copy(); t=1.0
    for it in range(iters):
       hy=response_weights(w,y)
       hc=hy-hy.mean()
       grad=2.0*response_weights(w,hc)
       gn=float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
       if gn<1e-13: break
       x=project_simplex(y - step*grad)
       # x sum1
       tn=0.5*(1.0+math.sqrt(1.0+4.0*t*t))
       y=x + ((t-1.0)/tn)*(x-a)
       ys=y.sum()
       if ys>0 and np.isfinite(ys): y=y/ys
       else: y=x
       a=x
       t=tn
       if it % 20 == 19:
           # optional restart if y far? keep
    return normalize_sequence(a)
```
Need import math.

FISTA `y` uses previous `a` (last prox). Standard. But if `y` becomes outside simplex due numerical, normalize. `x` projected exactly sum1. `a` last x.

We can include an objective check: If `it > 5 and it % 10 == 0`, compute objective? Not necessary. If FISTA oscillates, maybe PG. Could use plain PG with restart? Let's consider. FISTA for convex composite with projection should be fine. But our objective may not be differentiable? It is. Use `step=0.5`.

Could `response_weights(w,hc)` have w with sum1 but `hc` sum zero; B hc maybe. Good.

`blend_best`:
```
def blend_best(a,b, ts=(1.0,0.75,0.5,0.25,0.1)):
   a=normalize_sequence(a); if a is None: return None,inf
   best=a.copy(); bestsc=local_score(best)
   b=normalize_sequence(b); if b is None: return...
   for tt in ts:
      c=(1-tt)*a + tt*b
      c=normalize_sequence(c)
      if c is None: continue
      sc=local_score(c)
      if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<bestsc:
         best=c.copy(); bestsc=sc
   return best,bestsc
```
This recomputes score for t=0 each call; okay. To avoid repeated, maybe pass current score. Not needed.

Pseudo:
```
def kkt_optimize(a0, budget, seed, K=None):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+float(budget)
    a=normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a is None: return None,inf
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-15); a=normalize_sequence(a)
    best=a.copy(); bestsc=local_score(best)
    n=len(a)
    if K is None:
       K=int(min(max(80, n//4), 512))
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    lam_iters = 18 if n<=1024 else 8
    qp_iters = 45 if n<=2048 else 70
    stuck=0; it=0; mode_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       c=conv_same(a)
       M=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else inf
       if not np.isfinite(M) or M<=0: break
       active=active_top(c,K,32)
       cact=c[active]
       improved=False
       # determine lambdas to try
       lam_list=[]
       lamf,hf=solve_lambda_flat(a, active, cact, lam_iters)
       if lamf is not None: lam_list.append(lamf)
       s=float(np.sum(cact))
       if s>0 and np.isfinite(s): lam_list.append(cact/s)
       if it % 4 == 2 or stuck > 5:
          lam_list.append(np.ones(len(active))/len(active))
       if it % 9 == 8:
          rr=np.exp(rng.normal(size=len(active)))
          lam_list.append(rr/rr.sum())
       # Limit to 3 modes, but if budget large 4
       max_modes=3 if n<=1024 else 2
       if it==0: max_modes=2
       for mode,lam in enumerate(lam_list[:max_modes]):
          rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
          if rem < 3.0: break
          lam=project_simplex(lam)
          if lam is None? 
          w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[active]=lam
          # adaptive qp_iters by rem? 
          a_qp=qp_fix_lambda(a, w, qp_iters)
          if a_qp is None: continue
          b,b1b2sc=blend_best(a,a_qp,(1.0,0.7,0.4,0.15))
          if b1b2sc < bestsc:
              best=b.copy(); bestsc=b1b2sc; a=best.copy(); improved=True
              print(...); break
       if not improved:
          stuck+=1
          if stuck % 10 == 0:
              K = int(np.clip(K * (1.2 if stuck%20==0 else 0.8), max(32, n//12), min(1024,2*n-1)))
              rs = 0.05 + 0.2*rng.random()
              noise=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, rs, n))
              a=normalize_sequence(best * noise)
              if a is None: a=best.copy()
              a=np.maximum(a,1e-15); a=normalize_sequence(a)
              sc=local_score(a)
              if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < bestsc:
                  best=a.copy(); bestsc=sc
              stuck=0
       else:
          stuck=0
       it+=1
       if it % 40 ==0: print(f"kkt n={n} it={it} best={bestsc:.6f} stuck={stuck} t={time...}")
    return best,bestsc
```

def smooth_minimize(a0, budget, seed):
    if not HAVE_MIN: return a0,best
    t0; deadline
    a=normalize_sequence(a0); best=a; bestsc=score
    x=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    betas=(4.0,10.0,25.0,60.0,150.0)
    for beta in betas:
       rem=deadline-time
       if rem<25: break
       # calibrate
       st=time; v,g=smooth_value_grad(x,beta); per=(time-st)/1.0; per=max(1e-4, per*1.5)
       alloc=min(rem-15, 60.0)
       if alloc<5: break
       maxfun=int(min(3000, max(50, alloc/per)))
       try:
           res=minimize(smooth_value_grad, x, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, options={'maxiter':maxfun,'maxfun':maxfun,'gtol':1e-7,'ftol':1e-12})
           x2=res.x
       except Exception:
           break
       if hasattr(res,'x'):
           a_new=softmax(np.clip(res.x,-100,100))
           sc=local_score(a_new)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<bestsc:
               best=a_new; bestsc=sc; x=np.clip(res.x,-100,100)
           else:
               # still maybe use x if smooth val lower? Could move to different basin but exact worse. Use x anyway? It might help next beta. But risk. Use x=res.x clipped.
               x=np.clip(res.x,-100,100)
    return best,bestsc
```

LP step:
```
def lp_step(a, seed):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return a, local_score(a)
   a=normalize_sequence(a); n=len(a)
   if n>1500: return a, local_score(a)
   c=conv_same(a); M=max
   if M<=0: return
   max_rows=2*n-1 if n<=512 else 1024
   if max_rows >=2*n-1: active=np.arange(2*n-1)
   else: active=np.argpartition(c, -max_rows)[-max_rows:]
   # Build A
   A=np.zeros((len(active),n))
   for idx,k in enumerate(active):
       s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(k,n-1)
       if s<=e:
          j=np.arange(s,e+1,dtype=int); A[idx,j]=a[k-j]
   rng=...
   cvec=-np.ones(n)+1e-8*rng.normal(size=n)
   bvec=np.full(len(active), M*(1+1e-8))
   res=linprog(cvec,A_ub=A,b_ub=bvec,bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n,method='highs',options={'presolve':True})
   if res.success:
      g=normalize_sequence(res.x)
      return blend_best(a,g,(1.0,0.7,0.4,0.15))
   return a,local_score(a)
```
Call in kkt? Maybe in main when stuck and budget. To avoid nested, not inside kkt. In `propose_candidate`, after KKT maybe call LP if best_score not below target and n<=1024. But LP could be slow. We'll include optional in final for parent length if rem >60 and score above 1.505.

Candidate `interpolate_parent`:
```
def interpolate_parent(parent, n, seed, sigma):
   p=normalize_sequence(parent)
   if p is None: return make_candidate(n,seed,None)
   if p.size==1: val=np.full(n,float(p[0]))
   else:
      old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size); new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
      val=np.interp(new, old, p)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   if sigma>0: val=val*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,n))
   val=np.maximum(val,1e-12); return normalize_sequence(val)
```

`make_candidate` with parent. Ensure no closure.

def build_weight_vector(n, active, lam, mix):
    L=2*n-1
    w=np.full(L, mix/L, dtype=float)
    if active.size and lam.size:

def conv_same(a):
   n=len(a)
   if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
   if n*n <= DIRECT_LIMIT: return np.convolve(a,a)
   size=_fft_len(2*n-1)
   X=_rfft(a,size); out=_irfft(X*X,size)[:2*n-1]; return out
```

Now, `make_candidate` with parent. If parent is None. Need avoid using global in helper? Pass parent. Use `rng.choice([-1.0,1.0], size=n)`; for n maybe 1? handle. Anti-sym for n=1: val ones.

```
def make_candidate(n, seed, parent=None):
    n=int(max(2,n))
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    kind=int(rng.integers(0,5))
    p=parent
    if p is not None:
       p=normalize_sequence(p)
    # if parent and kind 0 maybe interpolate with noise
    if p is not None and kind < 2:
       return interpolate_parent(p,n,seed, float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.4)))
    if kind == 0:
       val=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.8)),n))
    elif kind == 1:
       z=rng.choice([-1.0,1.0],size=n)
       if n>=2:
          idx1=np.arange(n//2)
          idx2=n-1-idx1
          z[idx2] = -z[idx1]
          if n%2==1: z[n//2]=0.0
       alpha=float(rng.uniform(0.3,0.8))
       val=(1.0+alpha*z)/float(n)
    elif kind == 2:
       nb=int(rng.integers(max(4,n//64), max(5,n//16)+1))
       nb=int(np.clip(nb,1,n))
       h=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.5,nb)); h=h/float(np.mean(h))
       val=np.repeat(h, n//nb+1)[:n]
       val=val*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.2,n))
    elif kind == 3:
       val=rng.beta(2.0,2.0,n)
    else:
       val=np.ones(n)
    val=np.maximum(val,1e-12)
    return normalize_sequence(val)
```
If n=2, kind1 idx1 length1, idx2 length1. Good.

`interpolate_parent` should handle parent length maybe 1.

```
def interpolate_parent(parent,n,seed,sigma=0.1):
    p=normalize_sequence(parent)
    if p is None: return make_candidate(n,seed,None)
    if p.size == n:
       val=p.copy()
    else:
       old=np.linspace(0,1,p.size); new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       val=np.interp(new, old, p)
    if sigma and sigma>0:
       rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
       val=val*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,float(sigma),n))
    val=np.maximum(val,1e-12)
    return normalize_sequence(val)
```

- Use λ from flat or c, compute h=response(w,best), dir=h-mean; line search multiplicative. We can use `line_search_direction` top-level. This is cheap and can polish. Let's implement `direction_line_search(a, w, etas=8)`? Actually direction uses h centered. Top-level:
```
def multiplicative_line_search(a, w, max_evals=12):
   h=response_weights(w,a)
   hc=h-h.mean()
   norm=float(np.linalg.norm(hc))
   if norm<1e-300: return a, local_score(a)
   d=hc/norm
   best=a; bestsc=local_score(a)
   for eta in np.geomspace(0.02, 20, max_evals):
      b=normalize_sequence(a*np.exp(-np.clip(eta*d,-50,50)))
      if b is None: continue
      sc=local_score(b)
      if sc<bestsc: best=b; bestsc=sc
   return best,bestsc
```
In KKT, after building w for a mode, we can also call multiplicative line search with that w (cheap, 12 convolve) maybe before/after QP. But we already line search QP blend. Multiplicative may find smaller steps. Use if QP no improve? It adds cost. Maybe when stuck >3, try one multiplicative with flat w. Good.

```
def _fft_len(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    if HAVE_SCIPY_FT:
        try:
            return int(_next_len(m))
        except Exception:
            pass
    size=1
    while size < m:
        size <<= 1
    return size
```

`local_score` uses `a = np.asarray(a,float); a=np.clip...`; if contains nan, set 0.

```
def local_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return float('inf')
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300: return float('inf')
    a=a/s
    c=conv_same(a)
    c=np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    m=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float('inf')
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0: m=0.0
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * m / (s * s))
```
We normalize a for score; scale invariance. But if caller uses a sum1, okay. This recomputes s but then divides; if we normalize, s_new=1. We can use s original. Thus formula with original s: 2n*m_norm/s^2? Wait if a_norm=a/s, conv m_norm=m_orig/s². Score=2n*m_norm/(1²)=2n*m_orig/s². If we compute with m_norm and original s incorrectly 2n*m_norm/s² =2n*m_orig/s^4. Need be careful. In local_score, after normalizing a to sum1, we can just compute `score=2*n*m` (since sum=1). ```
def qp_fix_lambda(a0,w,iters,step=None):
   if step is None:
      # row sums
      cs=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(w)))
      # w length 2n-1, cs length 2n
      row = cs[n:] - cs[:n]  # length n
      rmax=float(np.max(row)) if row.size else 0.0
      L=max(1e-12, 2.0*rmax*rmax)
      step=1.0/L
      step=min(20.0, max(step,0.1))
```

It can call make_candidate if parent none; make_candidate defined maybe after? In Python, functions can call later-defined at runtime as long defined before invocation. We'll order make_candidate before interpolate_parent to be safe. `interpolate_parent` uses make_candidate.

In `make_candidate`, if parent is not None kind<2 calls interpolate_parent, which calls make_candidate if p None -> recursion? If parent None, p is None, so no. If p valid, no recursion. But if p invalid and make_candidate calls interpolate? We set p=normalize_sequence(parent); if p None, skip. Good.

`interpolate_parent` calls `make_candidate` if p None; make_candidate with parent None won't call interpolate. Fine.

Now, in FISTA, if step large, `x_new=project(y - step*grad)` could be far; projection stable. The objective should not increase if step≤1/L. Bound safe. Good.

```python
"""
Search for low-score nonnegative step-function coefficients.

Objective (after normalization sum(a)=1):
    score = 2n * max(convolve(a, a))

This program mainly uses an alternating KKT-style scheme:
  * choose a set of active convolution lags,
  * choose nonnegative dual weights on those lags,
  * solve a convex quadratic subproblem in a that flattens the
    dual action B(a), where B is the banded convolution operator
    induced by the dual weights,
  * accept only if the exact score improves.

Auxiliary tools include a softmax-smoothed max approximation with
L-BFGS-B, multipulative mirrored line searches, an LP step, and
interpolation/random restarts for different lengths.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_SCIPY_OPT = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY_OPT = False

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_MINIMIZE = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_MINIMIZE = False

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft as _rfft, irfft as _irfft, next_fast_len as _next_len
    HAVE_SCIPY_FT = True
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft as _rfft, irfft as _irfft
    HAVE_SCIPY_FT = False

DIRECT_LIMIT = 1_200_000


def _fft_len(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    if HAVE_SCIPY_FT:
        try:
            return int(_next_len(m))
        except Exception:
            pass
    size = 1
    while size < m:
        size <<= 1
    return size


def convolve_full(x, y):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    nx = x.size
    ny = y.size
    if nx == 0 or ny == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
    if nx * ny <= DIRECT_LIMIT:
        return np.convolve(x, y)
    size = _fft_len(nx + ny - 1)
    X = _rfft(x, size)
    Y = _rfft(y, size)
    out = _irfft(X * Y, size)
    return out[:nx + ny - 1]


def conv_same(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    if n * n <= DIRECT_LIMIT:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    size = _fft_len(2 * n - 1)
    X = _rfft(a, size)
    out = _irfft(X * X, size)
    return out[:2 * n - 1]


def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return a / s


def local_score(a):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return float("inf")
    c = conv_same(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m)


def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v.copy()
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u) - 1.0
    ind = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = (u - cssv / ind) > 0.0
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    rho = int(ind[cond][-1])
    theta = cssv[cond][rho - 1] / rho
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        out = out / s
    return out


def response_weights(w, a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if w.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if not float(np.sum(w)):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    full = convolve_full(a, w[::-1])
    stop = 2 * n - 1
    if full.size < stop:
        full = np.pad(full, (0, stop - full.size), mode="constant")
    return full[n - 1:stop][::-1]


def active_top(c, K, min_count=16):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    L = c.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.int64)
    K = int(min(max(1, K), L))
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    if idx.size < min_count:
        min_count = int(min(min_count, L))
        if min_count > idx.size:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -min_count)[-min_count:]
    return np.sort(idx.astype(np.int64))


def build_weight_vector(n, active, lam, mix):
    L = 2 * n - 1
    w = np.full(L, mix / float(L), dtype=np.float64) if mix > 0.0 else np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    if active.size > 0 and lam.size == active.size:
        w[active] += (1.0 - mix) * lam
    return w


def solve_lambda_flat(a, active, cact, iters=20):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = int(active.size)
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return None, None
    cact = np.asarray(cact, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    cact = np.where(np.isfinite(cact), cact, 0.0)
    cact = np.maximum(cact, 0.0)
    s = float(cact.sum())
    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        lam = cact / s
    else:
        lam = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    lam = project_simplex(lam)

    s2 = float(np.dot(a, a))
    Lbound = max(1e-12, 2.0 * float(m) * max(s2, 1e-12))
    step = 1.0 / Lbound
    step = min(20.0, max(step, 1e-8))

    best_lam = lam.copy()
    best_hc = None
    best_obj = float("inf")

    w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    for _ in range(max(1, int(iters))):
        w[active] = lam
        h = response_weights(w, a)
        hc = h - float(np.mean(h))
        obj = float(np.dot(hc, hc))
        if obj < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj
            best_lam = lam.copy()
            best_hc = hc.copy()
        if obj < 1e-24:
            break
        t = convolve_full(hc, a)
        grad = 2.0 * t[active]
        lam_new = project_simplex(lam - step * grad)
        if np.allclose(lam_new, lam, rtol=1e-13, atol=1e-13):
            break
        lam = lam_new
    return best_lam, best_hc


def qp_fix_lambda(a0, w, iters=45):
    a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a0.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a is None:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if w.size != 2 * n - 1:
        ww = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(w.size, ww.size)
        ww[:m] = w[:m]
        w = ww

    cs = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(w)))
    row = cs[n:] - cs[:n]
    rmax = float(np.max(row)) if row.size else 0.0
    Lbound = max(1e-12, 2.0 * rmax * rmax)
    step = 1.0 / Lbound
    step = min(20.0, max(step, 0.1))

    x = a.copy()
    y = a.copy()
    t = 1.0
    for _ in range(max(1, int(iters))):
        hy = response_weights(w, y)
        hy = hy - float(np.mean(hy))
        grad = 2.0 * response_weights(w, hy)
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            break
        x_new = project_simplex(y - step * grad)
        tn = 0.5 * (1.0 + math.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t * t))
        y = x_new + ((t - 1.0) / tn) * (x_new - x)
        ysum = float(y.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(ysum) or ysum <= 0.0:
            y = x_new
        else:
            y = y / ysum
        x = x_new
        t = tn
    return normalize_sequence(x)


def blend_best(a, b, ts=(1.0, 0.7, 0.4, 0.15, 0.05)):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    b = normalize_sequence(b)
    best = a.copy()
    bestsc = local_score(best)
    if b is None:
        return best, bestsc
    for tt in ts:
        c = (1.0 - float(tt)) * a + float(tt) * b
        c = normalize_sequence(c)
        if c is None:
            continue
        sc = local_score(c)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < bestsc:
            best = c.copy()
            bestsc = float(sc)
    return best, bestsc


def multiplicative_line_search(a, w, max_evals=10):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    h = response_weights(w, a)
    hc = h - float(np.mean(h))
    norm = float(np.linalg.norm(hc))
    best = a.copy()
    bestsc = local_score(best)
    if norm < 1e-300:
        return best, bestsc
    d = hc / norm
    etas = np.geomspace(0.02, 10.0, max(3, int(max_evals)))
    for eta in etas:
        for sign in (-1.0, 1.0):
            fac = np.clip(sign * float(eta) * d, -50.0, 50.0)
            b = normalize_sequence(a * np.exp(fac))
            if b is None:
                continue
            sc = local_score(b)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < bestsc:
                best = b.copy()
                bestsc = float(sc)
    return best, bestsc


def kkt_optimize(a0, budget, seed, K=None):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget))
    a = normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return None, float("inf")
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    best = a.copy()
    bestsc = local_score(best)
    if not np.isfinite(bestsc):
        return best, bestsc

    if K is None:
        K = int(min(max(80, n // 4), 512))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    lam_iters = 18 if n <= 1024 else 8
    qp_iters = 45 if n <= 1024 else (65 if n <= 2048 else 80)
    mix = 0.05

    stuck = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        c = conv_same(a)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break
        active = active_top(c, K, 32)
        m = int(active.size)
        if m == 0:
            active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64)
            m = 1
            cact = c[active]
        else:
            cact = c[active]

        improved = False
        primary_w = None

        lam_list = []
        lamf, _ = solve_lambda_flat(a, active, cact, lam_iters)
        if lamf is not None:
            lam_list.append((project_simplex(lamf), "flat"))

        scact = float(np.sum(cact))
        if scact > 0.0 and np.isfinite(scact):
            lam_list.append((project_simplex(cact / scact), "c"))

        lam_list.append((np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m), "u"))
        if it % 8 == 7:
            rr = np.exp(rng.normal(size=m))
            lam_list.append((project_simplex(rr / float(rr.sum())), "r"))

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 3.0:
            break
        max_modes = 3 if n <= 1024 else 2
        if rem < 12.0:
            max_modes = 1

        for lam, name in lam_list[:max_modes]:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 2.0:
                break
            w = build_weight_vector(n, active, lam, mix)
            if primary_w is None:
                primary_w = w
            a_qp = qp_fix_lambda(a, w, qp_iters)
            if a_qp is None:
                continue
            b, bsc = blend_best(a, a_qp, (1.0, 0.7, 0.4, 0.15, 0.05))
            if b is not None and np.isfinite(bsc) and bsc < bestsc:
                best = b.copy()
                bestsc = float(bsc)
                a = best.copy()
                improved = True
                print("kkt improve n={} mode={} score={:.6f} t={:.1f}".format(n, name, bestsc, time.monotonic() - t0), flush=True)
                break

        if not improved and primary_w is not None:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem > 5.0:
                b, bsc = multiplicative_line_search(a, primary_w, 8)
                if b is not None and np.isfinite(bsc) and bsc < bestsc:
                    best = b.copy()
                    bestsc = float(bsc)
                    a = best.copy()
                    improved = True
                    print("kkt mult improve n={} score={:.6f} t={:.1f}".format(n, bestsc, time.monotonic() - t0), flush=True)

        if improved:
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck % 10 == 0:
                K = int(min(max(32, int(K * (1.25 if (stuck // 10) % 2 == 0 else 0.8))), min(1024, 2 * n - 1)))
                sigma = 0.05 + 0.15 * float(rng.random())
                noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
                a = normalize_sequence(best * noise)
                if a is None:
                    a = best.copy()
                a = np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
                a = normalize_sequence(a)
                sc = local_score(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < bestsc:
                    best = a.copy()
                    bestsc = float(sc)
                stuck = 0

        it += 1
        if it % 40 == 0:
            print("kkt n={} it={} best={:.6f} stuck={} K={} t={:.1f}".format(
                n, it, bestsc, stuck, K, time.monotonic() - t0), flush=True)

    return best, bestsc


def softmax(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    mx = float(np.max(x))
    ex = np.exp(x - mx)
    s = float(ex.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(x.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)
    return ex / s


def smooth_value_grad(x, beta):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    a = softmax(x)
    c = conv_same(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf"), np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    beta = max(1e-3, float(beta))
    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = beta * (c - m)
    ez = np.exp(np.clip(z, -80.0, 80.0))
    s = float(ez.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        val = m
        w = np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        val = m + float(np.log(s)) / beta
        w = ez / s
    h = response_weights(w, a)
    grad_a = 2.0 * h
    ga_dot = float(np.dot(a, grad_a))
    grad_x = a * (grad_a - ga_dot)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad_x)):
        grad_x = np.zeros_like(x, dtype=np.float64)
    return float(val), grad_x


def smooth_minimize(a0, budget, seed):
    if not HAVE_MINIMIZE:
        return a0, local_score(a0)
    a = normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget))
    best = a.copy()
    bestsc = local_score(best)
    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    betas = (4.0, 12.0, 30.0, 80.0, 200.0)

    for beta in betas:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 25.0:
            break
        v0, g0 = smooth_value_grad(x, beta)
        if not np.isfinite(v0):
            break
        st = time.monotonic()
        _ = smooth_value_grad(x, beta)
        per = max(1e-5, (time.monotonic() - st) * 2.0)
        alloc = min(rem - 20.0, 70.0)
        if alloc < 6.0:
            break
        maxfun = int(min(3000, max(60, alloc / per)))
        try:
            res = minimize(
                smooth_value_grad,
                x,
                args=(beta,),
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=True,
                options={"maxiter": maxfun, "maxfun": maxfun, "gtol": 1e-7, "ftol": 1e-12}
            )
        except Exception:
            break
        if not hasattr(res, "x") or res.x is None:
            continue
        x2 = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x2)):
            continue
        a2 = softmax(np.clip(x2, -100.0, 100.0))
        sc2 = local_score(a2)
        accept = False
        if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < bestsc:
            best = a2.copy()
            bestsc = float(sc2)
            accept = True
            print("smooth improve beta={:.1f} score={:.6f}".format(beta, bestsc), flush=True)
        elif np.isfinite(res.fun) and res.fun < v0 * (1.0 - 1e-5) - 1e-10:
            accept = True
        if accept:
            x = np.clip(x2, -100.0, 100.0)
    return best, bestsc


def lp_step(a0, seed):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY_OPT:
        return a0, local_score(a0)
    a = normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    n = a.size
    if n > 1024:
        return a, local_score(a)
    c = conv_same(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return a, local_score(a)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return a, local_score(a)

    max_rows = 2 * n - 1 if n <= 512 else 1024
    if max_rows >= c.size:
        active = np.arange(c.size, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        active = np.argpartition(c, -max_rows)[-max_rows:]
        active = np.sort(active.astype(np.int64))

    A = np.zeros((active.size, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for idx, k in enumerate(active):
        s = max(0, int(k) - n + 1)
        e = min(int(k), n - 1)
        if s <= e:
            j = np.arange(s, e + 1, dtype=np.int64)
            A[idx, j] = a[k - j]

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    cvec = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1e-8 * rng.normal(size=n)
    bvec = np.full(active.size, M * (1.0 + 1e-8), dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    try:
        res = linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=bvec, bounds=bounds, method="highs",
                      options={"presolve": True})
    except Exception:
        return a, local_score(a)
    if res is None or not res.success or res.x is None:
        return a, local_score(a)
    g = normalize_sequence(res.x)
    if g is None:
        return a, local_score(a)
    return blend_best(a, g, (1.0, 0.7, 0.4, 0.15, 0.05))


def make_candidate(n, seed, parent=None):
    n = int(max(2, n))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    kind = int(rng.integers(0, 6))

    p = parent
    if p is not None:
        p = normalize_sequence(p)

    if p is not None and kind < 2:
        return interpolate_parent(p, n, seed, float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45)))

    if kind == 0:
        sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.8))
        val = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
    elif kind == 1:
        z = rng.choice(np.array([-1.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64), size=n)
        if n >= 2:
            left = np.arange(n // 2, dtype=np.int64)
            right = n - 1 - left
            z[right] = -z[left]
            if n % 2 == 1:
                z[n // 2] = 0.0
        alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 0.8))
        val = (1.0 + alpha * z) / float(n)
    elif kind == 2:
        lo = max(4, n // 64)
        hi = max(lo + 1, n // 16)
        nb = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
        nb = int(min(max(1, nb), n))
        h = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, nb))
        h = h / float(np.mean(h))
        val = np.repeat(h, n // nb + 1)[:n]
        val = val * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.2, n))
    elif kind == 3:
        val = rng.beta(2.0, 2.0, n)
    elif kind == 4:
        val = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n))
    else:
        val = rng.exponential(1.0, size=n)

    val = np.maximum(val, 1e-12)
    return normalize_sequence(val)


def interpolate_parent(parent, n, seed, sigma=0.1):
    p = normalize_sequence(parent)
    if p is None:
        return make_candidate(n, seed, None)
    if p.size == n:
        val = p.copy()
    elif p.size == 1:
        val = np.full(n, float(p[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size, dtype=np.float64)
        new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(n), dtype=np.float64)
        val = np.interp(new, old, p)
    if sigma and sigma > 0.0:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        val = val * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), int(n)))
    val = np.maximum(val, 1e-12)
    return normalize_sequence(val)


def consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, score):
    if cand is not None and np.isfinite(score) and float(score) < float(best_score):
        return normalize_sequence(cand), float(score)
    return best_seq, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1500.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0.0:
        budget = 1500.0
    deadline = start + max(10.0, budget) - 15.0

    parent = None
    try:
        par = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if par is not None:
            parent = normalize_sequence(par)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    if parent is not None:
        ps = local_score(parent)
        if np.isfinite(ps):
            best_seq = parent.copy()
            best_score = float(ps)
            print("parent length={} score={:.6f}".format(best_seq.size, best_score), flush=True)

    seedcur = int(seed)
    if best_seq is None:
        for tr in range(4):
            cand = make_candidate(512, seedcur + tr * 101, None)
            sc = local_score(cand)
            best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
            seedcur += 17
        if best_seq is None:
            best_seq = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
            best_score = local_score(best_seq)

    base_n = best_seq.size

    # Primary work on the current best / parent length.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 60.0:
        alloc = min(0.34 * budget, rem - 10.0)
        if alloc > 25.0:
            cand, sc = kkt_optimize(best_seq, alloc, seedcur)
            old = best_score
            best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
            print("after primary kkt score={:.6f} length={} delta={:.6f}".format(best_score, best_seq.size, old - best_score), flush=True)
            seedcur += 12345

    # Smooth polish.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 90.0 and best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 1200:
        alloc = min(0.10 * budget, rem - 30.0)
        if alloc > 30.0:
            cand, sc = smooth_minimize(best_seq, alloc, seedcur + 777)
            best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
            print("after smooth score={:.6f} length={}".format(best_score, best_seq.size), flush=True)
            seedcur += 22222

    # Try longer lengths by interpolating the best known density.
    long_ns = []
    if best_seq.size <= 512:
        long_ns.append(1024)
        if budget >= 900.0:
            long_ns.append(2048)
    elif best_seq.size <= 1024 and budget >= 900.0:
        long_ns.append(2048)

    for idx, nx in enumerate(long_ns):
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 80.0:
            break
        alloc = min(0.20 * budget, rem - 25.0)
        if alloc < 30.0:
            break
        a0 = interpolate_parent(best_seq, nx, seedcur + 1000 + idx * 555, sigma=0.15 + 0.1 * idx)
        sc0 = local_score(a0)
        best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, a0, sc0)
        cand, sc = kkt_optimize(a0, alloc, seedcur + 2000 + idx * 999)
        best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
        print("length {} candidate score={:.6f} best={:.6f}".format(nx, sc, best_score), flush=True)
        seedcur += 55555

    # Random restarts at original base length.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 140.0:
        trials = 2 if rem > 400.0 else 1
        per = (rem - 20.0) / float(max(1, trials))
        for tr in range(trials):
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 50.0:
                break
            alloc = min(per, rem - 10.0)
            a0 = make_candidate(base_n, seedcur + 7000 + tr * 313, parent)
            sc0 = local_score(a0)
            best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, a0, sc0)
            if alloc > 25.0:
                cand, sc = kkt_optimize(a0, alloc, seedcur + 8000 + tr * 997)
                best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
                print("random {} restart score={:.6f} best={:.6f}".format(base_n, sc, best_score), flush=True)
            seedcur += 99999

    # LP step on moderately sized layouts.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if HAVE_SCIPY_OPT and rem > 100.0 and best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 1024 and best_score > 1.5020:
        cand, sc = lp_step(best_seq, seedcur + 9000)
        best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
        print("after LP score={:.6f} length={}".format(best_score, best_seq.size), flush=True)
        seedcur += 11111

    # Final polish with whatever time remains.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None and rem > 40.0:
        cand, sc = kkt_optimize(best_seq, rem - 5.0, seedcur + 30000)
        best_seq, best_score = consider_candidate(best_seq, best_score, cand, sc)
        print("final kkt score={:.6f} length={}".format(best_score, best_seq.size), flush=True)

    final = normalize_sequence(best_seq)
    if final is None:
        if parent is not None:
            final = normalize_sequence(parent)
        else:
            final = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0

    try:
        official = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final]))
        if not np.isfinite(official):
            if parent is not None:
                fp = normalize_sequence(parent)
                if fp is not None:
                    try:
                        off2 = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in fp]))
                    except Exception:
                        off2 = float("inf")
                    if np.isfinite(off2):
                        final = fp
                        official = off2
            if final is None or not np.isfinite(official):
                final = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
                official = local_score(final)
    except Exception:
        official = local_score(final)

    final = np.asarray(final, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    final = np.where(np.isfinite(final), final, 0.0)
    final = np.maximum(final, 0.0)
    s = float(final.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        final = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0
    else:
        final = final / s

    print("FINAL local={} length={}".format(official, final.size), flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in final]
```
